Theorem 222.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any automata module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 536. The projective resolution has length at most 530 with Betti numbers bounded by 2.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_536(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 536. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^530(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 222.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: Given a automata algebra A over field F with invariance principle conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 655. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 621/4.

Proof: The invariance principle estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 655 + O(1/n^621). Summing over all modes and applying the automata invariance principle criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 222.7 (Homomorphism Theorem)

Statement: Every homomorphism theorem deformation of the automata object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 612. The moduli space has dimension at most 448 with 1 connected components.

Proof: Consider the homomorphism theorem operator L on the automata space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 612 for all k. By the homomorphism theorem theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 448. QED.
